color: rgb(44, 58, 74);" data-original-title="Reply type">Official Comment</span><span class="signatures">by <span>Authors</span></span><span class="created-date" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" data-original-title="Date created"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-calendar " aria-hidden="true"></span>26 Nov 2025, 08:32 (modified: 13 Feb 2026, 01:36)</span><span class="readers" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" data-original-title="Visible to &lt;br/&gt;everyone"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-eye-open " aria-hidden="true"></span>Everyone</span><span class="revisions"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-duplicate " aria-hidden="true"></span><a href="/revisions?id=GU3267deGY">Revisions</a></span></div><div class="note-content-container "><div class="note-content"><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Comment:</strong> <div class="note-content-value markdown-rendered"><p>We thank the reviewer for the follow-up. To ensure clarity and demonstrate that no "other comments" were left unaddressed, we provide the following point-by-point summary of revisions mapping your specific requests to our exact responses and changes in the paper:</p>
<blockquote>
<p>The theoretical results are limited to when both objective functions as well as the constraints are convex, which strongly limits their applicability.</p>
</blockquote>
<p>We added a theorem to the <strong>General Response part 3</strong>, extending our analysis to weakly convex objectives, following the standard in analyzing non-convex non-smooth scenarios.</p>
<blockquote>
<p>The results are obtained for when gradient descent (GD) is used for clients' local updates, which makes it more limited.</p>
</blockquote>
<p>We added a theorem to the <strong>General Response part 3</strong>, providing guarantees for Stochastic Gradient Descent (SGD).</p>
<blockquote>
<p>Although the work considers partial participation, the number of participating clients in each round is fixed to <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="264" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D45A TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>m</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container>, which highlights another limitation.</p>
</blockquote>
<p>We clarified that this is a standard notation convention (referencing SCAFFOLD, FedProx, FedOpt). Our analysis holds for variable participation by bounding with <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="265" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-msub><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D45A TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-script style="vertical-align: -0.15em;"><mjx-texatom size="s" texclass="ORD"><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c6D"></mjx-c><mjx-c class="mjx-c69"></mjx-c><mjx-c class="mjx-c6E"></mjx-c></mjx-mo></mjx-texatom></mjx-script></mjx-msub></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>m</mi><mrow data-mjx-texclass="ORD"><mo data-mjx-texclass="OP" movablelimits="true">min</mo></mrow></msub></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container>. Please find the detail in our original response.</p>
<blockquote>
<p>The experimental results in the main body of the paper are limited. The proposed approach is evaluated against only a centralized method (no FL).</p>
</blockquote>
<p>We added a comparison against <strong>Penalty-FedAvg</strong> (a heuristic FL baseline) on the NP Classification task (<a href="https://imgur.com/HPhwHK9" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">New: Figure 1</a>) and ADULT dataset (<a href="https://imgur.com/ppeEMIt" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">New: Figure 2</a>). Results show FedSGM outperforms the heuristic baseline significantly. As stated previously, please note that there are no existing FL baselines that handle general functional constraints, and Penalty-FedAvg was implemented specifically to address your request. In ADULT experiments (<a href="https://imgur.com/ppeEMIt" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">New: Figure 2</a>), we compared our result against three SOTA centralized baselines, which serves as the empirical best.</p>
<blockquote>
<p>What is the most valuable deliverable of this work? Is it the developed theory for when we have all the compression, partial participation, constraints on objectives and multiple local updates? If so, despite being valuable, the applicability of the results is still limited.</p>
</blockquote>
<p>The core deliverable of this work is to unlock the potential for realistic edge deployment with safety constraints. Our work is the first to provide a safe, fair algorithm that can be deployed on bandwidth-limited, unreliable edge networks with provable global optimality and feasibility guarantees.</p>
<blockquote>
<p>Introducing non-convexity and SGD will make the analysis harder, but more applicable. Im wondering if the authors have explored these directions? and what is the difficulty?</p>
</blockquote>
<p>We accepted this challenge and have successfully completed the analysis. We added two theorems in the <strong>General Response part 3</strong> with rigorous proofs handling the complexity of stochastic gradients and weakly convex objectives. Additionally, we conducted new fair classification experiment on ADULT dataset (<a href="https://imgur.com/ppeEMIt" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">New: Figure 2</a>). The primary challenge in this setting is the absence of unique minimizer which is not guaranteed in non-convex settings. We overcame this by utilizing classical Moreau envelope based analysis for weakly convex functions.</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>New comment:</strong> I would like to thank the authors for their response. I have read the comments and decide to keep my score. Although the work has some contributions, it still needs to be improved, e.g. in experimental results to fully support the theoretical results. Authors could also address my other comments as well. Therefore, I will keep my current score.</p>
</blockquote>
<p>We ask the reviewer for clarification on what the reviewer means about <strong>experimental results to fully support the theoretical results.</strong> For our NP Classification experiment (Figure 1 in the main paper), both the objective and the constraint are convex, and the results fully supports the theoretical claims made in the main paper. Similarly, in Figure 2 (in the main paper), the ablation study shows the impact of each component of our theoretical result, and we explained in detail in <strong>Discussion: NP Classification</strong> in page 7 of the main paper.</p>
<p>Additionally, to show the robustness of our algorithm in general deep learning scenarios, we tested on RL and ADULT dataset experiments. These deep-learning objectives are not assumed to be weakly convex. This separation between theoretical assumptions (for convergence proofs) and empirical evaluation (on general non-convex problems) follows the standard practice in optimization research.</p>
<p>We respectfully request that the reviewer specify which <strong>remaining points they feel were not addressed</strong>. We believe we have comprehensively resolved every concern raised in the initial review, specifically through the addition of new experiments and theoretical proofs.</p>
</div></div></div></div><div class="ForumReplyForm_container__w2VwR"><div class="ForumReplyForm_buttons__eGXo_"><span class="ForumReplyForm_hint__azwm6">Add:</span><button type="button" class="ant-btn css-1enej14 css-var-_R_5b_ ant-btn-primary ant-btn-color-primary ant-btn-variant-solid ant-btn-sm" style="font-size: 0.75rem; background-color: rgb(62, 103, 117); border: 2px solid rgb(63, 105, 120);"><span>Public Comment</span></button></div></div></div></div></div><div class="note  depth-odd" data-id="pnBHdsVgMh"><div class="btn-group-vertical btn-group-xs collapse-controls-v" role="group" aria-label="折叠控件"><button type="button" class="btn btn-default ">−</button><button type="button" class="btn btn-default middle ">＝</button><button type="button" class="btn btn-default active">≡</button></div><div class="heading"><h4><span>Official Review of Submission22751 by Reviewer RqPZ</span></h4><button type="button" class="btn btn-xs permalink-btn" aria-label="复制备注 pnBHdsVgMh 的 URL"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-link " data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" aria-hidden="true" data-original-title="Copy URL of note pnBHdsVgMh"></span><span class="sr-only">Copy URL of note pnBHdsVgMh</span></button></div><div class="subheading"><span class="invitation highlight" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" style="background-color: rgb(255, 187, 187); color: rgb(44, 58, 74);" data-original-title="Reply type">Official Review</span><span class="signatures">by <span>Reviewer RqPZ</span></span><span class="created-date" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" data-original-title="Date created"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-calendar " aria-hidden="true"></span>03 Nov 2025, 08:25 (modified: 12 Nov 2025, 18:12)</span><span class="readers" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" data-original-title="Visible to &lt;br/&gt;everyone"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-eye-open " aria-hidden="true"></span>Everyone</span><span class="revisions"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-duplicate " aria-hidden="true"></span><a href="/revisions?id=pnBHdsVgMh">Revisions</a></span></div><div class="note-content-container "><div class="note-content"><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Summary:</strong> <div class="note-content-value markdown-rendered"><p>This paper introduces FEDSGM, a unified framework for federated constrained optimization designed to simultaneously address four major challenges: functional constraints, communication bottlenecks, multiple local updates, and partial client participation. Proposed approach is built on the Switching Gradient Method (SGM). FEDSGM provides projection-free, primal-only updates, avoiding the need for expensive dual-variable tuning. To manage communication limits, it incorporates bidirectional error feedback to correct bias from compression, and its theoretical analysis explicitly models the interaction between this compression noise and the client drift from multiple local steps. The authors derive convergence guarantees showing the averaged iterate achieves the canonical <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="266" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-texatom texclass="ORD"><mjx-mi class="mjx-cal mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c4F TEX-C"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-texatom><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c28"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mn class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c31"></mjx-c></mjx-mn><mjx-texatom texclass="ORD"><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c2F"></mjx-c></mjx-mo></mjx-texatom><mjx-msqrt><mjx-sqrt><mjx-surd><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c221A"></mjx-c></mjx-mo></mjx-surd><mjx-box style="padding-top: 0.169em;"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D447 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-box></mjx-sqrt></mjx-msqrt><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c29"></mjx-c></mjx-mo></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mrow data-mjx-texclass="ORD"><mi data-mjx-variant="-tex-calligraphic" mathvariant="script">O</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>1</mn><mrow data-mjx-texclass="ORD"><mo>/</mo></mrow><msqrt><mi>T</mi></msqrt><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> rate, with high-probability bounds that isolate the sampling noise from partial participation. Additionally, a "soft switching" variant is proposed to stabilize updates near the feasibility boundary. The framework's efficacy is validated empirically on Neyman-Pearson classification and constrained Markov decision process (CMDP) tasks.</p>
</div></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Soundness:</strong> <span class="note-content-value">3: good</span></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Presentation:</strong> <span class="note-content-value">3: good</span></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Contribution:</strong> <span class="note-content-value">3: good</span></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Strengths:</strong> <div class="note-content-value markdown-rendered"><ol>
<li>The paper provides a rigorous and extensive theoretical analysis of its framework.</li>
<li>Well-motivated problems and applications.</li>
</ol>
</div></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Weaknesses:</strong> <div class="note-content-value markdown-rendered"><ol>
<li><p>Lack of Empirical Validation: The authors have not compared their proposed method with the state-of-the-art. The experiments (Section 4) only compare FEDSGM against a "Centralized" (i.e., non-federated) version of itself which is an ablation study.</p>
</li>
<li><p>It demonstrates that the federated setting introduces a performance cost (which is expected) but tells us nothing about whether FEDSGM is better than any other existing method.</p>
</li>
<li><p>Communication Efficiency:  In this paper, the authors does not propose a new communication-efficient technique. It merely "incorporates" standard  methods like Top-K compression and Error Feedback. Moreover, their method added new communication overhead. Algorithm 1 explicitly requires a "Constraint query" (lines 3-4) where all clients send their constraint value <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="267" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-msub><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D454 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-script style="vertical-align: -0.15em;"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D457 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-script></mjx-msub><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c28"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-msub><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D464 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-script style="vertical-align: -0.15em;"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D461 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-script></mjx-msub><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c29"></mjx-c></mjx-mo></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msub><mi>g</mi><mi>j</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi>w</mi><mi>t</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> to the server, which then aggregates and broadcasts the switching decision. This is a full, separate communication round-trip that must happen before the main gradient/model update. This added synchronisation step directly contradicts the goal of reducing communication bottlenecks.</p>
</li>
</ol>
</div></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Questions:</strong> <div class="note-content-value markdown-rendered"><ol>
<li>I want to see a detailed comparative analysis with SOTA (e.g., Federated Frank-Wolfe, and other projection-free methods ) to prove the superiority of the proposed approach.</li>
</ol>
</div></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Flag For Ethics Review:</strong> <span class="note-content-value">No ethics review needed.</span></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Rating:</strong> <span class="note-content-value">4: marginally below the acceptance threshold. But would not mind if paper is accepted</span></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Confidence:</strong> <span class="note-content-value">3: You are fairly confident in your assessment. It is possible that you did not understand some parts of the submission or that you are unfamiliar with some pieces of related work. Math/other details were not carefully checked.</span></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Code Of Conduct:</strong> <span class="note-content-value">Yes</span></div></div></div><div class="ForumReplyForm_container__w2VwR"><div class="ForumReplyForm_buttons__eGXo_"><span class="ForumReplyForm_hint__azwm6">Add:</span><button type="button" class="ant-btn css-1enej14 css-var-_R_5b_ ant-btn-primary ant-btn-color-primary ant-btn-variant-solid ant-btn-sm" style="font-size: 0.75rem; background-color: rgb(62, 103, 117); border: 2px solid rgb(63, 105, 120);"><span>Public Comment</span></button></div></div><div class="note-replies"><div class="note  depth-even" data-id="nORTPdDXWv"><div class="btn-group-vertical btn-group-xs collapse-controls-v" role="group" aria-label="折叠控件"><button type="button" class="btn btn-default ">−</button><button type="button" class="btn btn-default middle ">＝</button><button type="button" class="btn btn-default active">≡</button></div><div class="heading"><h4><strong>Response to Reviewer RqPZ</strong></h4><button type="button" class="btn btn-xs permalink-btn" aria-label="复制备注 nORTPdDXWv 的 URL"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-link " data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" aria-hidden="true" data-original-title="Copy URL of note nORTPdDXWv"></span><span class="sr-only">Copy URL of note nORTPdDXWv</span></button></div><div class="subheading"><span class="invitation highlight" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" style="background-color: rgb(187, 187, 255); color: rgb(44, 58, 74);" data-original-title="Reply type">Official Comment</span><span class="signatures">by <span>Authors</span></span><span class="created-date" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" data-original-title="Date created"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-calendar " aria-hidden="true"></span>22 Nov 2025, 01:35 (modified: 13 Feb 2026, 01:36)</span><span class="readers" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" data-original-title="Visible to &lt;br/&gt;everyone"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-eye-open " aria-hidden="true"></span>Everyone</span><span class="revisions"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-duplicate " aria-hidden="true"></span><a href="/revisions?id=nORTPdDXWv">Revisions</a></span></div><div class="note-content-container "><div class="note-content"><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Comment:</strong> <div class="note-content-value markdown-rendered"><p>We would like to thank the reviewer RqPZ and are encouraged that they found our work rigorous and extensive. We acknowledge that this problem is extremely timely and well motivated, particularly given the increasing need to balance performance with fairness and safety constraints in modern distributed learning systems.</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Weakness (1) and Question (1)</strong>: <em>Empirical validation and baselines...</em></p>
</blockquote>
<p>We have addressed the reviewer's concern on comparative analysis as follows:</p>
<ol>
<li><p><strong>Infeasibility of Frank-Wolfe/Projection Baselines:</strong> As detailed in our General Response, methods like Federated Frank-Wolfe or projected/proximal methods relies on solving optimization over a feasible set. For functional constraints defined in the distributed setting (<mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="268" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D454 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c28"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D464 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c29"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mo class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c3D"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mfrac space="4"><mjx-frac><mjx-num><mjx-nstrut></mjx-nstrut><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c31"></mjx-c></mjx-mn></mjx-num><mjx-dbox><mjx-dtable><mjx-line></mjx-line><mjx-row><mjx-den><mjx-dstrut></mjx-dstrut><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D45B TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-den></mjx-row></mjx-dtable></mjx-dbox></mjx-frac></mjx-mfrac><mjx-mo class="mjx-sop" space="2"><mjx-c class="mjx-c2211 TEX-S1"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-msub space="2"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D454 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-script style="vertical-align: -0.15em;"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D456 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-script></mjx-msub><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c28"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D464 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c29"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mo class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c2264"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c30"></mjx-c></mjx-mn></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>=</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><mi>n</mi></mfrac><mo data-mjx-texclass="OP">∑</mo><msub><mi>g</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>≤</mo><mn>0</mn></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container>), solving over a feaseible set requires an iterative subroutine where multiple inner iterations require multiple communication rounds with the server. Thus, Frank Wolfe is communication prohibitive for functional constraints, rendering a direct runtime comparison unfair.</p>
</li>
<li><p><strong>Comparison with Penalty-Based Baselines:</strong> The most viable alternative is a penalty-based approach (e.g., applying FedAvg on <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="269" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D453 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c28"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D465 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c29"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mo class="mjx-n" space="3"><mjx-c class="mjx-c2B"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" space="3"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D70C TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c5B"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D454 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c28"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D465 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c29"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-msub><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c5D"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-script style="vertical-align: -0.15em;"><mjx-mo class="mjx-n" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c2B"></mjx-c></mjx-mo></mjx-script></mjx-msub></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>f</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>+</mo><mi>ρ</mi><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>g</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>x</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><msub><mo stretchy="false">]</mo><mo>+</mo></msub></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container>). We have added a comparison against Penalty-FedAvg as in <a href="https://imgur.com/HPhwHK9" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">New: Figure 1</a>. The results demonstrate that penalty scheduling is a heuristic task without theoretical backing in this setting. As shown in the figure, small penalties yield infeasible solutions. Conversely, in order to emulate the theoretical hard constraint requires setting <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="270" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D70C TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>ρ</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> very large, which renders the problem ill-conditioned. This failure mode is explicitly visible in <a href="https://imgur.com/HPhwHK9" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">New: Figure 1</a> with the poor convergence for <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="271" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D70C TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c3D"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c31"></mjx-c><mjx-c class="mjx-c30"></mjx-c><mjx-c class="mjx-c30"></mjx-c></mjx-mn></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>ρ</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>100</mn></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container>. <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="272" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D439 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D452 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D451 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D446 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D43A TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D440 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>S</mi><mi>G</mi><mi>M</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> satisfies constraints without this manual tuning and the instability followed by it.</p>
</li>
</ol>
<p>To further strengthen our claim, we run additional experiments on ADULT dataset with SGD and heterogeneous data distribution, and compared the result against various centralized benchmarks (serving as the empirical best) to solve functional constrained problem.</p>
<ol start="3">
<li><strong>Comparison with Safe-EF:</strong> We clarify that Safe-EF is a special case of our framework under <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="273" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D438 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c3D"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c31"></mjx-c></mjx-mn></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> and full participation. Our ablation studies effectively cover this regime (comparing <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="274" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D438 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c3D"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c31"></mjx-c></mjx-mn></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>1</mn></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> vs <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="275" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D438 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c3E"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c31"></mjx-c></mjx-mn></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mn>1</mn></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container>), showing that our framework generalizes the Safe-EF to the more practical federated setting with partial participation and local updates, while refining their theoretical analysis.</li>
</ol>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Weakness (2)</strong>: <em>Comparison against other methods...</em></p>
</blockquote>
<p>We emphasize that <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="276" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D439 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D452 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D451 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D446 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D43A TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D440 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>S</mi><mi>G</mi><mi>M</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> is the first work to simultaneously address functional constraints, partial participation, local updates, and compression. As detailed in the General Response, existing methods in the literature (such as Federated Frank-Wolfe or Projection-based methods) are either mathematically incompatible with this setting or require prohibitive communication overhead (inner loops) to solve the functional constraints. Thus, a direct comparison to "existing methods" in the literature, which cannnot solve Problem (1), is not feasible.</p>
<p>To address the need for extra comparison, we constructed the Penalty-FedAvg baseline (see <a href="https://imgur.com/HPhwHK9" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">New: Figure 1</a>), which represents the closest adaptation of existing literature to this setting. The results confirm <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="277" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D439 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D452 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D451 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D446 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D43A TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-msup><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D440 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-script style="vertical-align: 0.363em; margin-left: 0.054em;"><mjx-mo class="mjx-var" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c2032"></mjx-c></mjx-mo></mjx-script></mjx-msup><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D460 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>S</mi><mi>G</mi><msup><mi>M</mi><mo data-mjx-alternate="1">′</mo></msup><mi>s</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> superiority over this adapted baseline. Additionally, we conducted an additional experiment on the ADULT dataset with data heterogeneity and SGD, validating that <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="278" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D439 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D452 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D451 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D446 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D43A TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D440 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>S</mi><mi>G</mi><mi>M</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> discovers feasible solutions in realistic settings and comparing against various other centralized baseline.</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Weakness (3)</strong>: <em>Communication Efficiency....</em></p>
</blockquote>
<p>We acknowledge that <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="279" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D439 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D452 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D451 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D446 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D43A TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D440 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>S</mi><mi>G</mi><mi>M</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> does not introduce a new compression mechanism. Our contribution, however, is a rigorous theoretical analysis of functional constraints in resource constrained FL setting. The novelty lies in the unification and proof that the four key challenges can coexist while maintaining convergence guarantees.</p>
<p>Regarding the "Constraint Query" overhead, we emphasize the distinction between Scalar Communication and Model Communication. The constraint query involves transmitting a single scalar (4 bytes) per client. This bandwidth cost is negligible compared to the model updates (megabytes/gigabytes) that we compress.
While this adds a synchronization step, it avoids the far greater cost of alternative methods. As described in the General Response, solving a functional constraint, for example by Frank-Wolfe, requires an iterative inner loop. This implies multiple rounds of vector communication per global step. Meanwhile, the overall bottleneck is drastically reduced by the use of bidirectional compression and multi-step local updates (<mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="280" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D438 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c3E"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c31"></mjx-c></mjx-mn></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi><mo>&gt;</mo><mn>1</mn></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container>), which far outweigh the minor cost of the constraint check involving scalar communication.</p>
</div></div></div></div><div class="ForumReplyForm_container__w2VwR"><div class="ForumReplyForm_buttons__eGXo_"><span class="ForumReplyForm_hint__azwm6">Add:</span><button type="button" class="ant-btn css-1enej14 css-var-_R_5b_ ant-btn-primary ant-btn-color-primary ant-btn-variant-solid ant-btn-sm" style="font-size: 0.75rem; background-color: rgb(62, 103, 117); border: 2px solid rgb(63, 105, 120);"><span>Public Comment</span></button></div></div></div></div></div><div class="note  depth-odd" data-id="l1mJYoGJlW"><div class="btn-group-vertical btn-group-xs collapse-controls-v" role="group" aria-label="折叠控件"><button type="button" class="btn btn-default ">−</button><button type="button" class="btn btn-default middle ">＝</button><button type="button" class="btn btn-default active">≡</button></div><div class="heading"><h4><span>Official Review of Submission22751 by Reviewer BCyy</span></h4><button type="button" class="btn btn-xs permalink-btn" aria-label="复制备注 l1mJYoGJlW 的 URL"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-link " data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" aria-hidden="true" data-original-title="Copy URL of note l1mJYoGJlW"></span><span class="sr-only">Copy URL of note l1mJYoGJlW</span></button></div><div class="subheading"><span class="invitation highlight" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" style="background-color: rgb(255, 187, 187); color: rgb(44, 58, 74);" data-original-title="Reply type">Official Review</span><span class="signatures">by <span>Reviewer BCyy</span></span><span class="created-date" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" data-original-title="Date created"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-calendar " aria-hidden="true"></span>01 Nov 2025, 21:16 (modified: 12 Nov 2025, 18:12)</span><span class="readers" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" data-original-title="Visible to &lt;br/&gt;everyone"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-eye-open " aria-hidden="true"></span>Everyone</span><span class="revisions"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-duplicate " aria-hidden="true"></span><a href="/revisions?id=l1mJYoGJlW">Revisions</a></span></div><div class="note-content-container "><div class="note-content"><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Summary:</strong> <div class="note-content-value markdown-rendered"><p>The paper proposes a unified theoretical framework for federated learning optimization that jointly captures several key aspects: constraints, partial participation, local computation, and bidirectional communication compression with error feedback. The framework generalizes many existing FL methods and, if it indeed recovers their best-known convergence rates across all settings (as claimed), it would constitute a strong theoretical contribution. The paper centers on the functional constraints and considers both hard and soft switching for satisfying them.</p>
</div></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Soundness:</strong> <span class="note-content-value">3: good</span></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Presentation:</strong> <span class="note-content-value">1: poor</span></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Contribution:</strong> <span class="note-content-value">3: good</span></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Strengths:</strong> <div class="note-content-value markdown-rendered"><ul>
<li><p>The unification of these aspects of federated learning (local steps, communication compression &amp; error feedback, partial participation) under one framework is meaningful and relevant. If the framework recovers the best-known convergence rates for all covered scenarios of literature (as claimed), it is worth publishing on its own.</p>
</li>
<li><p>The authors provide high-probability convergence guarantees for both hard and soft switching.</p>
</li>
</ul>
</div></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Weaknesses:</strong> <div class="note-content-value markdown-rendered"><p><strong>Constraint formulation</strong>: The novelty and significance of the constraint formulation seem to be overstated. Assumption 3 restricts the generality of the framework, limiting the scope of the unification.
Hard and soft switching closely resemble well-known approaches for minimizing an unconstrained regularized objective, with the regularizer R chosen as: R(w)=0 if G(w)&lt;=\eps and \infty otherwise (see the formulation [1]). In this viewpoint, the literature review seems to be inadequate.</p>
<blockquote>
<p>[1] Condat, Laurent, and Peter Richtárik. "Murana: A generic framework for stochastic variance-reduced optimization." Mathematical and Scientific Machine Learning. PMLR, 2022.</p>
</blockquote>
<p><strong>Practical relevance:</strong>
Practical relevance of the unified framework is unclear: FL is a diverse field, and it is not evident which real-world FL setups require all of the newly-enabled components simultaneously.</p>
<p><strong>Experimental comparison:</strong>
Experiments are limited and mostly illustrative. I think that there is a lot of missed potential. Having the unified framework, one can also compare the different aspects of the framework to answer fundamental questions of federated learning -- what is more important: to have more clients in partial participation, or to have more bits in the compresion schemes, or to do more local steps?</p>
<p><strong>Presentation issues:</strong> The paper needs a major revision before considering publishing.  There are missing definitions (convexity, Lipschitz continuity), a lack of formal rigor, and a disjointed and difficult-to-follow narrative.</p>
</div></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Questions:</strong> <div class="note-content-value markdown-rendered"><ul>
<li><p>How is the proposed notion of hard/soft constraint switching connected to projection (or inexact projection) onto the feasible set {w:G(w)&lt;= \eps}?</p>
</li>
<li><p>Is there a practically significant configuration (e.g., combination of compression, partial participation, local steps, and constraints) that is new and not covered by prior FL works?</p>
</li>
<li><p>Can the authors provide insights (both theoretical and empirical) on the relative importance of <strong>i)</strong> number of clients in partial participation, <strong>ii)</strong> number of bits in compression, and <strong>iii)</strong>* number of local updates?</p>
</li>
</ul>
</div></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Flag For Ethics Review:</strong> <span class="note-content-value">No ethics review needed.</span></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Rating:</strong> <span class="note-content-value">2: reject, not good enough</span></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Confidence:</strong> <span class="note-content-value">3: You are fairly confident in your assessment. It is possible that you did not understand some parts of the submission or that you are unfamiliar with some pieces of related work. Math/other details were not carefully checked.</span></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Code Of Conduct:</strong> <span class="note-content-value">Yes</span></div></div></div><div class="ForumReplyForm_container__w2VwR"><div class="ForumReplyForm_buttons__eGXo_"><span class="ForumReplyForm_hint__azwm6">Add:</span><button type="button" class="ant-btn css-1enej14 css-var-_R_5b_ ant-btn-primary ant-btn-color-primary ant-btn-variant-solid ant-btn-sm" style="font-size: 0.75rem; background-color: rgb(62, 103, 117); border: 2px solid rgb(63, 105, 120);"><span>Public Comment</span></button></div></div><div class="note-replies"><div class="note  depth-even" data-id="T5rfZIKzYK"><div class="btn-group-vertical btn-group-xs collapse-controls-v" role="group" aria-label="折叠控件"><button type="button" class="btn btn-default ">−</button><button type="button" class="btn btn-default middle ">＝</button><button type="button" class="btn btn-default active">≡</button></div><div class="heading"><h4><strong>Response to Reviewer BCyy (Part 1/2)</strong></h4><button type="button" class="btn btn-xs permalink-btn" aria-label="复制备注 T5rfZIKzYK 的 URL"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-link " data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" aria-hidden="true" data-original-title="Copy URL of note T5rfZIKzYK"></span><span class="sr-only">Copy URL of note T5rfZIKzYK</span></button></div><div class="subheading"><span class="invitation highlight" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" style="background-color: rgb(187, 187, 255); color: rgb(44, 58, 74);" data-original-title="Reply type">Official Comment</span><span class="signatures">by <span>Authors</span></span><span class="created-date" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" data-original-title="Date created"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-calendar " aria-hidden="true"></span>22 Nov 2025, 01:39 (modified: 13 Feb 2026, 01:36)</span><span class="readers" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" data-original-title="Visible to &lt;br/&gt;everyone"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-eye-open " aria-hidden="true"></span>Everyone</span><span class="revisions"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-duplicate " aria-hidden="true"></span><a href="/revisions?id=T5rfZIKzYK">Revisions</a></span></div><div class="note-content-container "><div class="note-content"><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Comment:</strong> <div class="note-content-value markdown-rendered"><p>We thank the reviewer for recognizing that our unification of federated challenges is "meaningful, relevant, and worth publishing on its own." <strong>The reviewer specifically noted that "the work is valuable if it recovers best-known convergence rates."</strong> We confirm that <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="281" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D439 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D452 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D451 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D446 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D43A TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D440 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>S</mi><mi>G</mi><mi>M</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> recovers the best-known canonical optimization rates of <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="282" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-texatom texclass="ORD"><mjx-mi class="mjx-cal mjx-b"><mjx-c class="mjx-c4F TEX-CB"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-texatom><mjx-mrow space="2"><mjx-mo class="mjx-lop"><mjx-c class="mjx-c28 TEX-S2"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mfrac><mjx-frac><mjx-num><mjx-nstrut></mjx-nstrut><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c31"></mjx-c></mjx-mn></mjx-num><mjx-dbox><mjx-dtable><mjx-line></mjx-line><mjx-row><mjx-den><mjx-dstrut></mjx-dstrut><mjx-msqrt size="s"><mjx-sqrt><mjx-surd><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c221A"></mjx-c></mjx-mo></mjx-surd><mjx-box style="padding-top: 0.169em;"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D447 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-box></mjx-sqrt></mjx-msqrt></mjx-den></mjx-row></mjx-dtable></mjx-dbox></mjx-frac></mjx-mfrac><mjx-mo class="mjx-lop"><mjx-c class="mjx-c29 TEX-S2"></mjx-c></mjx-mo></mjx-mrow></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mrow data-mjx-texclass="ORD"><mi data-mjx-variant="-tex-bold-calligraphic" mathvariant="bold-script">O</mi></mrow><mrow data-mjx-texclass="INNER"><mo data-mjx-texclass="OPEN">(</mo><mfrac><mn>1</mn><msqrt><mi>T</mi></msqrt></mfrac><mo data-mjx-texclass="CLOSE">)</mo></mrow></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> while decoupling the estimation error from the high-probability analysis for all covered scenarios. Furthermore, we have significantly expanded the applicability of our framework by adding new theoretical analyses for Stochastic Gradients (SGD) and <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="283" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D70C TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>ρ</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container>-Weakly Convex objectives (see General Response) in the Appendix D and E respectively. These additions demonstrate that our unified framework not only recovers standard rates in convex settings but also extends robustly to modern non-convex scenarios.</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Weakness (1)</strong>: <em>Constraint formulation....</em></p>
</blockquote>
<p>We thank the reviewer for pointing out the connection to regularized optimization and the connection to MURANA paper. The reviewer's intuition is right in saying that one could construct an indicator function based on the constraint feasibility and this reformulation would lead to the mathematically equivalent optimization problem. However, this reformulation does not yield a feasible algorithm for FL as we are solving the problem of functional constraints. MURANA relies on the proximal operator of <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="284" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D445 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c28"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D464 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c29"></mjx-c></mjx-mo></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>R</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container>. As detailed in our General Response, the proximal operator for a functional constraint corresponds to the Euclidean Projection onto the feasible set. In a federated setting, computing this projection requires an iterative inner loop involving multiple rounds of vector communication per global step. Thus, while the mathematical formulations are equivalent, the proximal algorithms are communication restrictive. The novelty of <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="285" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D439 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D452 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D451 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D446 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D43A TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D440 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>S</mi><mi>G</mi><mi>M</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> lies in developing a primal-only switching framework that satisfies the constraint while avoiding the massive communication cost of the proximal operator that makes standard proximal methods inapplicable. We kindly refer the reviewer to see General Response for a more formal and extensive treatment of not only MURANA(projection) but Frank-Wolfe as well as Penalty method.</p>
<p><strong>On Literature Review:</strong> We have expanded our literature review to explicitly include the discussion in General Response and it is now present in the Appendix (G).</p>
<p><strong>On Assumption 3:</strong>
We clarify that Assumption 3 is not a limitation of our framework, but rather the standard theoretical characterization of Client Heterogeneity in partial participation settings. In unconstrained FL, a standard assumption widely used in the analysis is bounded client dissimilarity or bounded global variance, i.e, <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="286" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-texatom texclass="ORD"><mjx-mi class="mjx-ds mjx-b"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D53C TEX-A"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-texatom><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c5B"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c2225"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mi class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c2207"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-msub><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D453 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-script style="vertical-align: -0.15em; margin-left: -0.06em;"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D456 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-script></mjx-msub><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c28"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D464 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c29"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mo class="mjx-n" space="3"><mjx-c class="mjx-c2212"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mi class="mjx-n" space="3"><mjx-c class="mjx-c2207"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D453 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c28"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D464 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c29"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-msup><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c2225"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-script style="vertical-align: 0.363em;"><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c32"></mjx-c></mjx-mn></mjx-script></mjx-msup><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c5D"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mo class="mjx-n" space="4"><mjx-c class="mjx-c2264"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-msup space="4"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D70E TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-script style="vertical-align: 0.363em;"><mjx-mn class="mjx-n" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c32"></mjx-c></mjx-mn></mjx-script></mjx-msup></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mrow data-mjx-texclass="ORD"><mi mathvariant="double-struck">E</mi></mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mo data-mjx-texclass="ORD">∥</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">∇</mi><msub><mi>f</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>−</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">∇</mi><mi>f</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><msup><mo data-mjx-texclass="ORD">∥</mo><mn>2</mn></msup><mo stretchy="false">]</mo><mo>≤</mo><msup><mi>σ</mi><mn>2</mn></msup></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container>. In constrained FL with functional constraints, it is necessary to impose an analogous bound on the heterogeneity of the constraint function <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="287" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D454 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c28"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D464 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c29"></mjx-c></mjx-mo></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> across clients. Assumption 3, which states that <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="288" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-texatom texclass="ORD"><mjx-mover><mjx-over style="padding-bottom: 0.105em; padding-left: 0.476em; margin-bottom: -0.531em;"><mjx-mo class="mjx-n" style="width: 0px; margin-left: -0.25em;"><mjx-c class="mjx-c5E"></mjx-c></mjx-mo></mjx-over><mjx-base><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D43A TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-base></mjx-mover></mjx-texatom><mjx-mo class="mjx-n" space="3"><mjx-c class="mjx-c2212"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" space="3"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D454 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mrow data-mjx-texclass="ORD"><mover><mi>G</mi><mo stretchy="false">^</mo></mover></mrow><mo>−</mo><mi>g</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> is sub-Gaussian, is simply the high-probability equivalent of this standard dissimilarity bound present in literature, such as FedAVG, SCAFFOLD, FedProx, etc., and applied to <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="289" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D454 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>g</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container>. Without bounding this heterogeneity, estimating the global constraint <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="290" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mo class="mjx-sop"><mjx-c class="mjx-c2211 TEX-S1"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-msub space="2"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D454 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-script style="vertical-align: -0.15em;"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i" size="s"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D456 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-script></mjx-msub><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c28"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D464 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c29"></mjx-c></mjx-mo></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mo data-mjx-texclass="OP">∑</mo><msub><mi>g</mi><mi>i</mi></msub><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>w</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> from a subset of clients would be theoretically impossible (as the variance would be unbounded). Thus, this assumption defines its validity in FL problems rather than restricting the algorithm's theory.</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Weakness (2) and Question (2)</strong>: <em>Practical relevance....</em></p>
</blockquote>
<p>We thank the reviewer for asking the question about practical relevance. We argue that we are analyzing these four components in tandem not as a theoretical excercise but rather as the requirement for modern FL. The first three components of local updates, partial participation, and compression is a necessity in the field for resource constrained edge devices. A natural addition to the three components is the functional constraint. While previous works like MURANA analyzed constrained FL under set constraints, they cannot handle functional constraints required for modern ML objectives.</p>
<p>A simple yet concrete example of this practical necessity is mobile keyboard prediction, for e.g., Gboard, across millions of devices. The literature has already established that partial participation, local updates, and compression are mandatory to handle bandwidth and latency limits. However, modern deployments also demand fairness. For instance, ensuring the model does not degrade performance for minority language speakers requires enforcing a functional constraint (e.g. demographic parity). Our algorithm is the first to provide provable guarantees for this realistic scenario where efficiency and fairness constraints must coexist.</p>
<p><mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="291" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" class="mjx-c1D44E TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D45F TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D461 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo </div></div></div></div><div class="ForumReplyForm_container__w2VwR"><div class="ForumReplyForm_buttons__eGXo_"><span class="ForumReplyForm_hint__azwm6">Add:</span><button type="button" class="ant-btn css-1enej14 css-var-_R_5b_ ant-btn-primary ant-btn-color-primary ant-btn-variant-solid ant-btn-sm" style="font-size: 0.75rem; background-color: rgb(62, 103, 117); border: 2px solid rgb(63, 105, 120);"><span>Public Comment</span></button></div></div></div><div class="note  depth-even" data-id="17iN5yxb1t"><div class="btn-group-vertical btn-group-xs collapse-controls-v" role="group" aria-label="折叠控件"><button type="button" class="btn btn-default ">−</button><button type="button" class="btn btn-default middle ">＝</button><button type="button" class="btn btn-default active">≡</button></div><div class="parent-title"><h5><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-share-alt " aria-hidden="true"></span> Replying to Response to Reviewer BCyy (Part 1/2)</h5></div><div class="heading"><h4><strong>Response to Reviewer BCyy (Part 2/2)</strong></h4><button type="button" class="btn btn-xs permalink-btn" aria-label="复制笔记 17iN5yxb1t 的 URL"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-link " data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" aria-hidden="true" data-original-title="Copy URL of note 17iN5yxb1t"></span><span class="sr-only">Copy URL of note 17iN5yxb1t</span></button></div><div class="subheading"><span class="invitation highlight" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" style="background-color: rgb(187, 187, 255); color: rgb(44, 58, 74);" data-original-title="Reply type">Official Comment</span><span class="signatures">by <span>Authors</span></span><span class="created-date" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" data-original-title="Date created"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-calendar " aria-hidden="true"></span>22 Nov 2025, 01:40 (modified: 13 Feb 2026, 01:36)</span><span class="readers" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" data-original-title="Visible to &lt;br/&gt;everyone"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-eye-open " aria-hidden="true"></span>Everyone</span><span class="revisions"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-duplicate " aria-hidden="true"></span><a href="/revisions?id=17iN5yxb1t">Revisions</a></span></div><div class="note-content-container "><div class="note-content"><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Comment:</strong> <div class="note-content-value markdown-rendered"><blockquote>
<p><strong>Weakness (3) and Question (3)</strong>: <em>Experimental comparison and theoretical insight....</em></p>
</blockquote>
<p>We thank the reviewer for this interesting perspective on the fundamental trade-offs. We view these factors through the lens of System Constraints vs. Algorithmic Control:</p>
<ol>
<li>System Constraints (Participation &amp; Compression): Although we control these with hyperparametrs, these are largely dictated by the system or environment; for example, client availability and communication bottleneck. In our case, with functional constraints, low participation induces a noise floor that slows the convergence. Our experiments (see NP CLassification Figure 2: Row 2) confirm that reducing participation causes frequent violation of the constraints while hindering the convergence.</li>
</ol>
<p>While compression is also a system constraint, our results show the algorithm is robust to it. Thanks to error feedback where noise is corrected over time rather than getting accumulated, we can handle aggressive compression with minimal performance loss, as observed in Figure 2: Row 3.</p>
<ol start="2">
<li>Algorithmic Control (Local Updates): Local updates (E) is a hyperparameter that can be dictated by the server. In our theoretical guarantees for both optimality and feasibility, we have <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="292" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-msqrt><mjx-sqrt><mjx-surd><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c221A"></mjx-c></mjx-mo></mjx-surd><mjx-box style="padding-top: 0.167em;"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D438 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-box></mjx-sqrt></mjx-msqrt></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><msqrt><mi>E</mi></msqrt></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> in the upper bound, capturing the effect of client drift. So, increasing <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="293" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D438 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>E</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> aggresively can harm more where the "Client Drift" error outweighs the communication savings, destabilizing the constraint satisfaction. We clearly observed this trade-off in our experiments see Figure 2: Row 1.</li>
</ol>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Weakness (4)</strong>: <em>Presentation....</em></p>
</blockquote>
<p>We sincerely thank the reviewer for holding our manuscript to a high standard. We have now revised the paper to address these concerns. We have now added the definition of convexity and Lipschtness in Assumption 1. Furthermore, we have added the discussion presented in General Response as part of the related work in Appendix (G) incorporating reviewers questions about MURANA. We believe these changes address the presentation concerns. We welcome specific pointers on any other concerns during the discussion period.</p>
</div></div></div></div><div class="ForumReplyForm_container__w2VwR"><div class="ForumReplyForm_buttons__eGXo_"><span class="ForumReplyForm_hint__azwm6">Add:</span><button type="button" class="ant-btn css-1enej14 css-var-_R_5b_ ant-btn-primary ant-btn-color-primary ant-btn-variant-solid ant-btn-sm" style="font-size: 0.75rem; background-color: rgb(62, 103, 117); border: 2px solid rgb(63, 105, 120);"><span>Public Comment</span></button></div></div></div></div></div><div class="note  depth-odd" data-id="VNzc0sQJCk"><div class="btn-group-vertical btn-group-xs collapse-controls-v" role="group" aria-label="折叠控件"><button type="button" class="btn btn-default ">−</button><button type="button" class="btn btn-default middle ">＝</button><button type="button" class="btn btn-default active">≡</button></div><div class="heading"><h4><span>Official Review of Submission22751 by Reviewer PoVG</span></h4><button type="button" class="btn btn-xs permalink-btn" aria-label="复制备注 VNzc0sQJCk 的 URL"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-link " data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" aria-hidden="true" data-original-title="Copy URL of note VNzc0sQJCk"></span><span class="sr-only">Copy URL of note VNzc0sQJCk</span></button></div><div class="subheading"><span class="invitation highlight" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" style="background-color: rgb(255, 187, 187); color: rgb(44, 58, 74);" data-original-title="Reply type">Official Review</span><span class="signatures">by <span>Reviewer PoVG</span></span><span class="created-date" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" data-original-title="Date created"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-calendar " aria-hidden="true"></span>30 Oct 2025, 04:58 (modified: 12 Nov 2025, 18:12)</span><span class="readers" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" data-original-title="Visible to &lt;br/&gt;everyone"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-eye-open " aria-hidden="true"></span>Everyone</span><span class="revisions"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-duplicate " aria-hidden="true"></span><a href="/revisions?id=VNzc0sQJCk">Revisions</a></span></div><div class="note-content-container "><div class="note-content"><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Summary:</strong> <div class="note-content-value markdown-rendered"><p>The paper proposes FEDSGM, a unified framework for federated constrained optimization that simultaneously handles four major challenges: (1) functional constraints, (2) bi-directional communication compression, (3) multiple local updates per round, and (4) partial client participation. Built upon the Switching Gradient Method, FEDSGM adopts a projection-free, primal-only update scheme that avoids dual variable tuning and inner-loop projections. The framework incorporates error-feedback mechanisms to correct compression bias and establishes an <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="294" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D442 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c28"></mjx-c></mjx-mo><mjx-mn class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c31"></mjx-c></mjx-mn><mjx-texatom texclass="ORD"><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c2F"></mjx-c></mjx-mo></mjx-texatom><mjx-msqrt><mjx-sqrt><mjx-surd><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c221A"></mjx-c></mjx-mo></mjx-surd><mjx-box style="padding-top: 0.169em;"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D447 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-box></mjx-sqrt></mjx-msqrt><mjx-mo class="mjx-n"><mjx-c class="mjx-c29"></mjx-c></mjx-mo></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>O</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>1</mn><mrow data-mjx-texclass="ORD"><mo>/</mo></mrow><msqrt><mi>T</mi></msqrt><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> convergence rate under convex objectives. Experiments on Neyman–Pearson classification and constrained MDPs demonstrate convergence and constraint satisfaction.</p>
</div></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Soundness:</strong> <span class="note-content-value">3: good</span></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Presentation:</strong> <span class="note-content-value">3: good</span></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Contribution:</strong> <span class="note-content-value">3: good</span></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Strengths:</strong> <div class="note-content-value markdown-rendered"><ol>
<li>The work is the first to combine constraint handling, compression, multiple local steps, and partial participation in a single theoretical framework.</li>
<li>The convergence proof carefully separates optimization and sampling errors and analyzes the bias introduced by compression and local drift. Both hard and soft switching variants are theoretically justified.</li>
<li>FEDSGM avoids inner projections and dual updates, leading to a lightweight and practical approach for resource-constrained federated systems.</li>
<li>Experiments on Neyman–Pearson classification and CMDP tasks confirm the feasibility and stability of the proposed method.</li>
</ol>
</div></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Weaknesses:</strong> <div class="note-content-value markdown-rendered"><ol>
<li>The theory assumes convex objectives and constraints, which restricts applicability to deep federated learning scenarios. The extension to nonconvex settings is not discussed.</li>
<li>Experiments are restricted to small datasets and tabular RL tasks. Results on large-scale or nonconvex benchmarks (e.g., image classification or language models) would greatly strengthen the empirical claims.</li>
<li>The experimental section lacks strong comparisons with recent constrained FL baselines (e.g., primal-dual or penalty-based methods), making it difficult to evaluate relative performance.</li>
<li>No experiments under heterogeneous data distributions.</li>
</ol>
</div></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Questions:</strong> <div class="note-content-value markdown-rendered"><p>See weaknesses above.</p>

Math/other details were not carefully checked.</span></div><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Code Of Conduct:</strong> <span class="note-content-value">Yes</span></div></div></div><div class="ForumReplyForm_container__w2VwR"><div class="ForumReplyForm_buttons__eGXo_"><span class="ForumReplyForm_hint__azwm6">Add:</span><button type="button" class="ant-btn css-1enej14 css-var-_R_5b_ ant-btn-primary ant-btn-color-primary ant-btn-variant-solid ant-btn-sm" style="font-size: 0.75rem; <blockquote>
<p><strong>Weakness (1)</strong>: <em>Non-convexity....</em></p>
</blockquote>
<p>We agree that limiting the analysis to convexity restricts applicability to deep learning. To address this, we have significantly expanded our theoretical analysis in the Appendix (D and E). As detailed in the General Response, we now provide a convergence proof for <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="295" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D70C TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>ρ</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container>-Weakly Convex objectives (a standard class of non-convex functions covering neural networks), demonstrating that <mjx-container class="MathJax CtxtMenu_Attached_0" jax="CHTML" tabindex="0" ctxtmenu_counter="296" style="font-size: 121.3%; position: relative;"><mjx-math class="MJX-TEX" aria-hidden="true"><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D439 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D452 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D451 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D446 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D43A TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi><mjx-mi class="mjx-i"><mjx-c class="mjx-c1D440 TEX-I"></mjx-c></mjx-mi></mjx-math><mjx-assistive-mml unselectable="on" display="inline"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><mi>F</mi><mi>e</mi><mi>d</mi><mi>S</mi><mi>G</mi><mi>M</mi></math></mjx-assistive-mml></mjx-container> is robust to non-convex setting.</p>
<blockquote>
<p><strong>Weakness (2), (3) and (4)</strong>: <em>Experimental restriction....</em></p>
</blockquote>
<p>We appreciate the suggestion to strengthen empirical validation. We first clarify that our existing RL experiment is not tabular, but utilizes Deep Neural Network policies in a continuous environment, inherently covering the non-convex and stochastic setting along with extreme data heterogeneity. We made this point clear and explicit in the revised paper.</p>
<p>To further address the call for heterogeneous data distributions (Weakness (4)) and strong baselines (Weakness (3)), we introduced a new fair classification experiment on ADULT dataset using a Deep NN under heterogeneous (non-IID) data distributions and stochastic gradients. Please refer to <a href="https://imgur.com/ppeEMIt" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">New: Figure 2</a>, Geneeral Response and Appendix (F).</p>
</div></div></div></div><div class="ForumReplyForm_container__w2VwR"><div class="ForumReplyForm_buttons__eGXo_"><span class="ForumReplyForm_hint__azwm6">Add:</span><button type="button" class="ant-btn css-1enej14 css-var-_R_5b_ ant-btn-primary ant-btn-color-primary ant-btn-variant-solid ant-btn-sm" style="font-size: 0.75rem; background-color: rgb(62, 103, 117); border: 2px solid rgb(63, 105, 120);"><span>Public Comment</span></button></div></div></div><div class="note  depth-even" data-id="Z85urJrRMF"><div class="btn-group-vertical btn-group-xs collapse-controls-v" role="group" aria-label="折叠控件"><button type="button" class="btn btn-default ">−</button><button type="button" class="btn btn-default middle ">＝</button><button type="button" class="btn btn-default active">≡</button></div><div class="parent-title"><h5><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-share-alt " aria-hidden="true"></span> Replying to Response to PoVG</h5></div><div class="heading"><h4><span>Official Comment by Reviewer PoVG</span></h4><button type="button" class="btn btn-xs permalink-btn" aria-label="复制笔记 Z85urJrRMF 的 URL"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-link " data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" aria-hidden="true" data-original-title="Copy URL of note Z85urJrRMF"></span><span class="sr-only">Copy URL of note Z85urJrRMF</span></button></div><div class="subheading"><span class="invitation highlight" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" style="background-color: rgb(187, 187, 255); color: rgb(44, 58, 74);" data-original-title="Reply type">Official Comment</span><span class="signatures">by <span>Reviewer PoVG</span></span><span class="created-date" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" data-original-title="Date created"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-calendar " aria-hidden="true"></span>26 Nov 2025, 01:07 (modified: 13 Feb 2026, 01:36)</span><span class="readers" data-toggle="tooltip" data-placement="top" title="" data-original-title="Visible to &lt;br/&gt;everyone"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-eye-open " aria-hidden="true"></span>Everyone</span><span class="revisions"><span class="glyphicon glyphicon-duplicate " aria-hidden="true"></span><a href="/revisions?id=Z85urJrRMF">Revisions</a></span></div><div class="note-content-container "><div class="note-content"><div><strong class="note-content-field disable-tex-rendering">Comment:</strong> <div class="note-content-value markdown-rendered"><p>I appreciate the authors' efforts during rebuttal, especially the new proofs and new experiments in Appendix.</p>
<p>However, I have some follow-up questions.</p>
